```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nOne-wash laundering refers to this concentrated signal loss at the 0v1 boundary: while depth-1 remains marginally distinguishable from depth-2 (1v2 $\\auc$ 0.61--0.70, the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ finding), the dominant distributional shift occurs at generation $0{\\to}1$, and beyond depth~2 no further signal is exploitable.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\nOne-wash laundering is distinct from model collapse~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse}: model collapse describes cumulative quality degradation across generations, while one-wash laundering describes the erasure of statistical fingerprints distinguishing adjacent generations; text quality may continue degrading even after the features separating generation $k$ from $k{+}1$ become indistinguishable.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ and OLMo yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 1$, consistent with its 15D boundary (Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).\nThe 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\\Kempirical{=}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.\nThe gap between $\\Kempirical$ and $\\Ktheory$ remains open; a DeBERTa-v3-base upper-bound probe yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = \\Kempirical^{\\text{OBD}} = 2$ on C4 and Code but $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = 4$ on arXiv, placing the cost of generator-agnostic features at one depth level for structured text and zero for web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_vs_obd}).\nFeature rankings are scorer-independent ($\\rho > 0.90$), but self-scoring inflates boundary AUC (OLMo: $+$11.5\\,pp, Pythia: $+$4.7\\,pp); cross-scoring provides a conservative estimate for deployment against unknown generators (Table~\\ref{tab:runtime}; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nC4 and Code consistently yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Wikipedia ranges from 1 to 2 depending on architecture; structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across four independent architecture families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), with OLMo-1B as the sole exception ($\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all domains; cross-scorer validated), as formulaic structure (citations, math notation, section conventions) creates larger per-generation distributional shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\\ ${\\sim}0.01$ for C4).\nThis elevation is not driven by \\LaTeX{} syntax: stripping markup preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ ($\\Delta\\auc = {-}0.021$); Code, despite rigid syntax, yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, confirming academic conventions rather than structural complexity as the source.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nRetrain-chain experiments across three families (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen-1.5B; LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (3v4 $\\auc = 0.68$--$0.80$); extending Pythia and Qwen to depth~7 sustains $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (6v7 $\\auc = 0.634$--$0.641 > \\theta$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts absent from text-only recycling.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines; our conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings.\n\n\\paragraph{Decoding strategy as confound.}\nGreedy decoding inflates depth separability: Pythia-1.4B greedy 1v2 $\\auc$ reaches $0.884$ versus $0.617$--$0.727$ for stochastic strategies ($\\Kempirical{=}2$ in all cases), driven by repetition collapse ($d_0{=}0.01 \\to d_1{=}0.72$ 4-gram repeat ratio; Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}).\n\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 20,
        "oldLines": 8,
        "newStart": 20,
        "newLines": 8,
        "lines": [
          " Feature rankings are scorer-independent ($\\rho > 0.90$), but self-scoring inflates boundary AUC (OLMo: $+$11.5\\,pp, Pythia: $+$4.7\\,pp); cross-scoring provides a conservative estimate for deployment against unknown generators (Table~\\ref{tab:runtime}; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}",
          "-C4 and Code consistently yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Wikipedia ranges from 1 to 2 depending on architecture; structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across four independent architecture families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), with OLMo-1B as the sole exception ($\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all domains; cross-scorer validated), as formulaic structure (citations, math notation, section conventions) creates larger per-generation distributional shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\\ ${\\sim}0.01$ for C4).",
          "-This elevation is not driven by \\LaTeX{} syntax: stripping markup preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ ($\\Delta\\auc = {-}0.021$); Code, despite rigid syntax, yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, confirming academic conventions rather than structural complexity as the source.",
          "+C4 and Code yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Wikipedia varies (1--2); arXiv yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across four families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), with OLMo-1B as the sole exception ($\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all domains; cross-scorer validated).",
          "+Formulaic structure creates larger per-generation shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\\ ${\\sim}0.01$ for C4); stripping markup preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ ($\\Delta\\auc = {-}0.021$), and Code yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ despite rigid syntax, confirming academic conventions as the source.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}",
          " Retrain-chain experiments across three families (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen-1.5B; LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (3v4 $\\auc = 0.68$--$0.80$); extending Pythia and Qwen to depth~7 sustains $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (6v7 $\\auc = 0.634$--$0.641 > \\theta$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts absent from text-only recycling."
        ]
```

```text
73	
74	\begin{figure}[!t]
75	\centering
76	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_transfer_heatmap.pdf}
77	\vspace{-6pt}
78	\caption{Cross-model transfer heatmap (continuation-mode models). Each cell shows 6-class ordinal accuracy when trained on the row model and tested on the column model, divided by in-domain accuracy (retention rate). Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo: 89--92\%.}
79	\label{fig:transfer_heatmap}
80	\end{figure}
81	
82	Continuation-mode classifiers transfer well across generators (Figure~\ref{fig:transfer_heatmap}; full matrix in Table~\ref{tab:s1_transfer}): Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo retains 89--92\%, Qwen-cont$\leftrightarrow$Pythia 84--85\%, Qwen-cont$\leftrightarrow$OLMo 80--85\%.
83	Cross-scale transfer from Gemma-2-27B retains $>$100\% accuracy on 1B--8B models (101.3\% avg; reverse: 76.5\%), suggesting larger models learn a universal depth-feature superset (Table~\ref{tab:gemma27b_transfer}).
84	Qwen-rewrite classifiers transfer poorly (61--63\%), confirming reliance on mode-specific artifacts rather than generalizable depth features.
85	
86	\paragraph{Cross-scorer validation.}
87	Replacing the self-scorer with Pythia-1.4B or OLMo-1B preserves feature rankings ($\rho > 0.90$) but attenuates absolute discriminability at the boundary (Table~\ref{tab:crossscorer}).
```

```text
I found the issues. There are several places in the appendix that reference "four model-mode configurations":

1. Line 67: "The four model-mode configurations (Qwen-rw, Pythia-cont, OLMo-cont, Qwen-cont)"
2. Line 73 (Table S4 caption): "the other three model-mode configurations (Pythia-cont, OLMo-cont, Qwen-cont)"
3. Line 336: "four model-mode configurations" in another table caption

The main text (method.tex line 54) says: "six primary configurations spanning three model families... 

So the total is actually 10 configurations: six primary ones (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct in rewrite and continuation modes, Qwen2.5-1.5B base, Qwen2.5-7B, Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B) plus four additional models introduced in Section 4 (LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B, Gemma-2-2B, Gemma-2-27B). The appendix references are inconsistent with this breakdown.

Looking more carefully at the appendix, it describes the C4 corpus construction using only four model-mode configurations that shared identical depth-0 seeds: Qwen-rw, Pythia-cont, OLMo-cont, and Qwen-cont. These four were the initial configurations used to build the generational depth corpus, while the other models came later for domain and scale experiments. The six primary configurations mentioned in the methods include the base Qwen2.5-1.5B and Qwen2.5-7B variants. Looking at the six primary configurations listed in the method section, I can see the appendix only covers four of them—it's missing the base Qwen2.5-1.5B and the Qwen2.5-7B models. The caption for Table S4 suggests the C4 corpus might have been constructed using only those four configurations, which would explain the discrepancy, but I need to verify whether all six models were actually evaluated on the same dataset. I need to locate Table 8 in the appendix to see what it actually shows and verify whether it matches the six primary configurations mentioned in the method section.
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01TJ1VHt9DyCxXaKLcekMzN2`</summary>

```text
345	\end{table}
346	
347	The asymmetry is interpretable: Gemma-2-27B generates more human-like text ($\Kempirical{=}1$, weaker depth signal), so a classifier trained on its subtle features easily transfers to smaller models with stronger signals.
348	Conversely, classifiers trained on coarser small-model features miss the fine-grained patterns needed to discriminate 27B-generated depths.
349	This confirms that depth features are text-intrinsic and scale-modulated rather than model-specific artifacts.
350	
351	\subsection{Multi-Source arXiv Validation}
352	\label{sec:arxiv_multi_source}
353	
354	Training a single OBD classifier on arXiv text pooled from three generators (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen-1.5B; Qwen cross-scored) yields $\Kempirical{=}3$ (2v3 $\auc = 0.657\;[0.647,\,0.667]$, 1v2 $= 0.858$; Bonferroni $p < 0.005$), matching single-generator arXiv results (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}).
355	The multi-source setting tests whether the arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$ elevation persists when generator identity is not controlled; the result confirms that the domain ceiling is text-intrinsic rather than architecture-specific.
356	
357	\section{Use of AI Assistants}
358	\label{app:ai-assistants}
359	
360	We used Claude (Anthropic) as an assistant for code debugging/refactoring, grammar-level editing, and formatting.
361	All scientific claims, experimental design, results, and citations were authored and verified by the authors.
362	
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "\\section{Use of AI Assistants}\n\\label{app:ai-assistants}",
  "new_string": "\\subsection{Cross-Scale Filtering: Gemma-2-27B}\n\\label{sec:gemma27b_filtering}\n\nTo test whether OBD filtering generalizes to unseen large-scale generators, we apply the Qwen-1.5B-trained binary classifier to Gemma-2-27B-generated text and fine-tune on the filtered corpus (9 runs, 3 strategies $\\times$ 3 seeds).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\footnotesize\n\\caption{Downstream performance when filtering Gemma-2-27B-generated text with a Qwen-1.5B-trained OBD classifier. Binary filtering does not degrade performance, confirming robustness to unknown large-scale generators.}\n\\label{tab:gemma27b_filtering}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Strategy} & \\textbf{MMLU} & \\textbf{HellaSwag} \\\\\n\\midrule\nNo filter          & 0.5956 & 0.6633 \\\\\nBinary-ours        & 0.5958 ($+$0.02\\,pp) & 0.6674 ($+$0.41\\,pp) \\\\\nGraduated          & 0.5953 ($-$0.03\\,pp) & 0.6629 ($-$0.04\\,pp) \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nBinary filtering retains or marginally improves both metrics ($+$0.02\\,pp MMLU, $+$0.41\\,pp HellaSwag), consistent with the Qwen-1.5B filtering results (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) and confirming that the OBD classifier transfers across generator scales without retraining.\n\n\\section{Use of AI Assistants}\n\\label{app:ai-assistants}"
}
```

```json
          "+\\caption{Downstream performance when filtering Gemma-2-27B-generated text with a Qwen-1.5B-trained OBD classifier. Binary filtering does not degrade performance, confirming robustness to unknown large-scale generators.}",
          "+\\label{tab:gemma27b_filtering}",
          "+\\begin{tabular}{lcc}",
          "+\\toprule",
          "+\\textbf{Strategy} & \\textbf{MMLU} & \\textbf{HellaSwag} \\\\",
          "+\\midrule",
          "+No filter          & 0.5956 & 0.6633 \\\\",
          "+Binary-ours        & 0.5958 ($+$0.02\\,pp) & 0.6674 ($+$0.41\\,pp) \\\\",
          "+Graduated          & 0.5953 ($-$0.03\\,pp) & 0.6629 ($-$0.04\\,pp) \\\\",
          "+\\bottomrule",
          "+\\end{tabular}",
          "+\\end{table}",
          "+",
          "+Binary filtering retains or marginally improves both metrics ($+$0.02\\,pp MMLU, $+$0.41\\,pp HellaSwag), consistent with the Qwen-1.5B filtering results (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) and confirming that the OBD classifier transfers across generator scales without retraining.",
          "+",
          " \\section{Use of AI Assistants}",
          " \\label{app:ai-assistants}",
          " "
        ]
      }
    ],
```

   - K*_empirical: discrimination boundary = max depth where AUC > θ=0.60

   - Bonferroni-corrected 95% CI for boundary pair validation
   - Cross-scorer validation: using different model to score features, confirms K* is not a scoring artifact

   - Gemma-2-27B filtering: OBD binary filtering robust to unseen 27B generators

```json
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nOne-wash laundering refers to this concentrated signal loss at the 0v1 boundary: while depth-1 remains marginally distinguishable from depth-2 (1v2 $\\auc$ 0.61--0.70, the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ finding), the dominant distributional shift occurs at generation $0{\\to}1$, and beyond depth~2 no further signal is exploitable.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\nOne-wash laundering is distinct from model collapse~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse}: model collapse describes cumulative quality degradation across generations, while one-wash laundering describes the erasure of statistical fingerprints distinguishing adjacent generations; text quality may continue degrading even after the features separating generation $k$ from $k{+}1$ become indistinguishable.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ and OLMo yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 1$, consistent with its 15D boundary (Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).\nThe 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\\Kempirical{=}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.\nThe gap between $\\Kempirical$ and $\\Ktheory$ remains open; a DeBERTa-v3-base upper-bound probe yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = \\Kempirical^{\\text{OBD}} = 2$ on C4 and Code but $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = 4$ on arXiv, placing the cost of generator-agnostic features at one depth level for structured text and zero for web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_vs_obd}).\nFeature rankings are scorer-independent ($\\rho > 0.90$), but self-scoring inflates boundary AUC (OLMo: $+$11.5\\,pp, Pythia: $+$4.7\\,pp); cross-scoring provides a conservative estimate for deployment against unknown generators (Table~\\ref{tab:runtime}; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nC4 and Code yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Wikipedia varies (1--2); arXiv yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across four families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), with OLMo-1B as the sole exception ($\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all domains; cross-scorer validated).\nFormulaic structure creates larger per-generation shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\\ ${\\sim}0.01$ for C4); stripping markup preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ ($\\Delta\\auc = {-}0.021$), and Code yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ despite rigid syntax, confirming academic conventions as the source.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nRetrain-chain experiments across three families (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen-1.5B; LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (3v4 $\\auc = 0.68$--$0.80$); extending Pythia and Qwen to depth~7 sustains $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (6v7 $\\auc = 0.634$--$0.641 > \\theta$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts absent from text-only recycling.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines; our conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings.\n\n\\paragraph{Decoding strategy as confound.}\nGreedy decoding inflates depth separability: Pythia-1.4B greedy 1v2 $\\auc$ reaches $0.884$ versus $0.617$--$0.727$ for stochastic strategies ($\\Kempirical{=}2$ in all cases), driven by repetition collapse ($d_0{=}0.01 \\to d_1{=}0.72$ 4-gram repeat ratio; Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}).\n\n",
        "numLines": 33,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 33
      }
    },
```

```text
169	\paragraph{Robustness.}
170	\label{sec:robustness}
171	$\Kempirical{=}2$ holds across segment lengths of 128, 256, and 512 tokens (Table~\ref{tab:s11_length_ablation}); at 512 tokens the 1v2 CI lower bound falls marginally below $\theta$ but 2v3 remains well below $\theta$ at all lengths ($\leq 0.572$).
172	Seed robustness testing (seed $= 123$ vs.\ primary $= 42$; Table~\ref{tab:seed_robustness}) confirms $\Kempirical$ stability in 2/3 configurations; the one marginal shift (Qwen-Wikipedia $\Kempirical{=}2 \to 3$, 2v3 $\auc = 0.607$) has one of five folds below $\theta$.
173	The ${\pm}9$\,pp absolute AUC variation across seeds strengthens the case for threshold-based $\Kempirical$ over raw AUC comparisons.
174	
175	%% ====================================================================
176	\subsection{Binary Detector Confidence}
177	\label{sec:binary_confidence}
178	
179	Binary detectors (DivEye~\cite{basani2026diveye}, Binoculars~\cite{hans2024binoculars}) provide no depth signal beyond the human-vs.-machine boundary: $\Kempirical^{\text{binary}} \leq 1$, with moderate rank correlations ($|\rho| = 0.06$--$0.46$) driven entirely by the 0v1 gap (Table~\ref{tab:s9_binary_spearman}).
```

Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps.
````text
# Bootstrap CI 引用审计报告

> 审计日期: 2026-05-18
> 背景: Bootstrap resampling 打破 GroupKFold 结构，导致数据泄漏（+6.4pp inflation on 1v2 AUC）。需确认论文正文中所有 bootstrap CI 引用已替换为 fold-based CI。

## 结论

**论文 .tex 源文件中仍有 13 处残留 bootstrap 引用描述我们自己的方法，全部需要修改为 fold-based CI。**

---

## 一、需要修改的位置（描述我们自己的方法）

### 1. docs/paper/method.tex — 第 25 行（核心方法论描述，最高优先级）

**当前文本:**
```
Bootstrap 95\% confidence intervals (CIs; 1{,}000 resamples\footnote{Boundary-pair results in Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci} use 1{,}000 resamples; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 for tighter bounds at the margin.}) provide uncertainty quantification: we verify that boundary-pair CI lower bounds exceed $\theta$ and non-boundary-pair CI upper bounds fall below $\theta$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
```

**建议替换为:**
```
We quantify uncertainty via fold-based 95\% confidence intervals (CIs) derived from the five GroupKFold splits: we verify that boundary-pair CI lower bounds exceed $\theta$ and non-boundary-pair CI upper bounds fall below $\theta$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
```
````

```text
128	\subsection{Cross-Domain Variation}
129	\label{sec:cross_domain}
130	
131	The preceding experiments use C4 (general web text).
132	To test domain dependence, we evaluate on Wikipedia ($n{=}5{,}000$) and arXiv abstracts ($n{=}2{,}200$) across three architecture families (Table~\ref{tab:s12_domain_ablation}).
133	Wikipedia yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ for Qwen-1.5B (1v2 $\auc = 0.619$; Table~\ref{tab:domain_kstar}), but $\Kempirical{=}1$ for LLaMA-8B and Mistral-7B (1v2 $\auc = 0.592,\,0.558$; both below $\theta$).
134	Qwen-1.5B, Pythia-1.4B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, and Mistral-7B yield $\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv (Table~\ref{tab:domain_kstar}): 2v3 $\auc = 0.644\;[0.629,\,0.659]$, $0.648\;[0.634,\,0.662]$, $0.622\;[0.605,\,0.635]$, and $0.628$ respectively (permutation $p < 0.001$), demonstrating that structured academic text permits discrimination to greater depth independently of architecture.
135	OLMo-1B is the sole exception ($\Kempirical{=}1$ across all domains; cross-scorer validated, $p{=}0.510$); Mistral-7B exhibits a monotonic staircase (Wiki${=}$1, C4${=}$1, Code${=}$2, arXiv${=}$3), confirming that $\Kempirical$ is domain-determined.
136	The elevated signal reflects higher per-generation distributional shift (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\ ${\sim}0.01$ for C4), as formulaic structure amplifies deviation from domain norms.
137	We additionally evaluate on CodeSearchNet Python ($n{=}5{,}000$, continuation mode); all four tested models (Qwen-1.5B, Qwen-7B, LLaMA-8B, Mistral-7B) yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ (Table~\ref{tab:domain_kstar}), matching the C4 baseline.
138	
139	\begin{table}[t]
```
